Let $A\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ be an infinite set. Is $$ \sum_{n\in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} $$ irrational?

Erdős problem 257: Let $A\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ be an infinite set. Imported from formal-conjectures (Apache-2.0) · erdosproblems.com/257

The problem's statement.

What is claimed

$\displaystyle \forall\, A \subseteq \mathbb{N}\ \text{infinite}:\qquad \sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

The sum is Lean's tsum over $A$, in $\mathbb{R}$; every such family is summable (its terms are at most $2^{1-n}$ for $n \ge 1$), so it is the ordinary sum. In Lean the term at $n = 0$ is $\frac{1}{2^0-1} = \frac10 = 0$, so a set containing $0$ has the same sum as the set without it; this is the affirmative reading of Erdős's question, with $A$ ranging over infinite sets of natural numbers.

unverified · history

Lean
theorem Opn.erdos_257 :
    ∀ (A : Set ℕ), A.Infinite →
    Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := by
  sorry

Split into

Proved from these holes by 20261020T203102Z-fable-1020-e257-c-partial.lean (merged in 3dbbb392ec95, attestation 000781).

  • h_greedy → erdos-257--h1 (proved)

    $\displaystyle 0 \notin A,\ \sum_{a\in A} \frac{1}{2^a-1} = r \in \mathbb{Q} \implies \forall n \ge 1:\ n \in A \iff \frac{1}{2^n-1} \le r - \sum_{a \in A,\, a<n} \frac{1}{2^a-1}$

  • h_core → erdos-257--h2 (open)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, r\in\mathbb{Q},\ \forall\, G\subseteq\mathbb{N}:\quad 0\notin G,\ \ \Bigl(\forall n\ge 1:\ n\in G \iff \frac{1}{2^n-1} \le r-\sum_{a\in G,\ a<n}\frac{1}{2^a-1}\Bigr),\ \ \sum_{a\in G}\frac{1}{2^a-1}=r \ \Longrightarrow\ G \text{ is finite}.$

Read the partial proof (Lean)
theorem Opn.erdos_257 :
    ∀ (A : Set ℕ), A.Infinite →
    Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := by
  -- annex: 91f974f5322a5af6fbb42be7d529c58fe7cd39798e06cb7ddd0cdd1f2975fbd6
  -- approach: greedy walk; a representation of a rational is greedy (bookkeeping), and the open core is that a greedy set summing to a rational is finite (expected false: the walk for 1/2 runs past 10^8)
  -- licence: CC-BY-4.0
  -- label: a representation of a rational is greedy
  -- claim: $0 \notin A,\ \sum_{a\in A} \frac{1}{2^a-1} = r \in \mathbb{Q} \implies \forall n \ge 1:\ n \in A \iff \frac{1}{2^n-1} \le r - \sum_{a \in A,\, a<n} \frac{1}{2^a-1}$
  -- Bookkeeping: if n ∈ A the remainder after the terms below n is the term at n plus a nonnegative tail;
  -- if n ∉ A it is the tail over a > n, which the gap lemma (spec-ddd99827) bounds strictly below the term at n.
  -- Uniqueness of representation (spec-5e2e0890) is a corollary of this and is the refutation side's lemma.
  have h_greedy : ∀ (r : ℚ) (A : Set ℕ), 0 ∉ A →
      ∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) = r →
      ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n → (n ∈ A ↔
        (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1) ≤ r - ∑' a : ↥(A ∩ Set.Iio n), (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ a.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: the greedy walk for a rational stalls (open core)
  -- claim: $0 \notin G,\ G \text{ greedy for } r \in \mathbb{Q},\ \sum_{a\in G} \frac{1}{2^a-1} = r \implies G \text{ finite}$
  -- The open core of Erdős 257. Expected FALSE: Kovač (erdosproblems.com/257, 2025) expects the fat Cantor set
  -- of subset sums to contain rationals, and the greedy walk for 1/2 runs without stalling to depth 10^8
  -- (Hong 2026, unrefereed). A proof of spec-a70fcca4 (1/2 is an infinite subset sum) refutes this hole and the root.
  -- The hypothesis ∑ G = r is essential: without it the greedy walk of a rational in a gap of the Cantor set
  -- runs forever with a sum below r, and the statement would be trivially false rather than the open question.
  have h_core : ∀ (r : ℚ) (G : Set ℕ), 0 ∉ G →
      (∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n → (n ∈ G ↔
        (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1) ≤ r - ∑' a : ↥(G ∩ Set.Iio n), (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ a.1 - 1))) →
      ∑' n : G, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) = r → G.Finite := sorry
  intro A hA
  rintro ⟨r, hr⟩
  have h0 : ∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) = ∑' n : ↥(A \ {0}), (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) := by
    have e1 : ∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)
        = ∑' n : ℕ, A.indicator (fun m : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m - 1)) n :=
      tsum_subtype A (fun m : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m - 1))
    have e2 : ∑' n : ↥(A \ {0}), (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)
        = ∑' n : ℕ, (A \ {0}).indicator (fun m : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m - 1)) n :=
      tsum_subtype (A \ {0}) (fun m : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m - 1))
    rw [e1, e2]
    congr 1
    funext x
    by_cases hx : x = 0
    · subst hx; simp [Set.indicator]
    · have hmem : x ∈ A \ {0} ↔ x ∈ A := by simp [hx]
      by_cases hxA : x ∈ A
      · rw [Set.indicator_of_mem hxA, Set.indicator_of_mem (hmem.mpr hxA)]
      · rw [Set.indicator_of_notMem hxA, Set.indicator_of_notMem (fun h => hxA (hmem.mp h))]
  have hA' : (A \ {0}).Infinite := hA.sdiff (Set.finite_singleton 0)
  have hsum : ∑' n : ↥(A \ {0}), (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) = r := by rw [← h0, hr]
  have hgr := h_greedy r (A \ {0}) (by simp) hsum
  exact hA' (h_core r (A \ {0}) (by simp) hgr hsum)

Split into

Proved from these holes by 20261023T133841Z-t1023-f1-partial.lean (merged in 6c84a6bd13c0, attestation 000955).

  • h_naturals → variant-2f5236aa (proved)

    $\displaystyle \sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_progressions → variant-886b66da (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, a, d \in \mathbb{N},\ d \ge 1:\qquad \sum_{m \in \{a + dn \,:\, n \ge 0\}} \frac{1}{2^m-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_periodic → variant-5274cf38 (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, A \subseteq \mathbb{N} \text{ infinite}:\quad \bigl(\exists\, p \ge 1,\ N:\ \forall n \ge N,\ n \in A \iff n + p \in A\bigr) \ \Longrightarrow\ \sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_coprime → variant-c91009b9 (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, A \subseteq \mathbb{N} \text{ infinite, pairwise coprime, with } \sum_{n\in A}\frac1n < \infty:\qquad \sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_squarefree → variant-970a2ffa (proved)

    $\displaystyle \sum_{n \ \text{squarefree}} \frac{1}{2^n-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_powers → variant-7a0f5735 (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, k \ge 1:\qquad \sum_{m \in \{n^k \,:\, n \in \mathbb{N}\}} \frac{1}{2^m-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_two_squares → variant-d08712d9 (proved)

    $\displaystyle \sum_{m \,\in\, \{a^2+b^2 \,:\, a,\, b \in \mathbb{N}\}} \frac{1}{2^m-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_primes → variant-6b364d2b (open)

    $\displaystyle \sum_{p \text{ prime}}\frac{1}{2^p-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

  • h_ratio_two → variant-d144ccc1 (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall A \subseteq \mathbb{N}:\quad A \text{ infinite},\ 0 \notin A,\ \bigl(\exists N\ \forall m, n \in A:\ N \le n < m \Rightarrow 2n \le m\bigr) \ \Longrightarrow\ \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}.$

  • h_liouville → variant-1629563f (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall A \subseteq \mathbb{N}:\quad A \text{ infinite},\ 0 \notin A,\ \Bigl(\forall C \in \mathbb{N}\ \exists m \in A\ \exists L \in \mathbb{N}:\ L > 0,\ \bigl(\forall a \in A,\ a < m \Rightarrow (2^a - 1) \mid L\bigr),\ 2^C L \le 2^m\Bigr) \ \Longrightarrow\ \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}.$

  • h_long_gaps → variant-41d1f19b (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall A \subseteq \mathbb{N}:\quad A \text{ infinite},\ 0 \notin A,\ \Bigl(\forall C \in \mathbb{N}\ \exists m \in A:\ C + \sum_{a \in A,\ a < m} a \le m\Bigr) \ \Longrightarrow\ \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}.$

  • h_two_and_three → variant-037e597d (proved)

    $\displaystyle \sum_{n \in \{2^k : k \in \mathbb{N}\} \cup \{3^k : k \in \mathbb{N}\}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}.$

  • h_two_bases → variant-f93b89d8 (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, a, b \in \mathbb{N},\quad 2 \le a < b \;\Longrightarrow\; \sum_{n \in \{a^k : k \in \mathbb{N}\} \cup \{b^k : k \in \mathbb{N}\}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}.$

  • h_two_chains → variant-77c0756e (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, x, y : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{N},\quad \Big(x_0, y_0 > 0,\ \ x_j \mid x_{j+1},\ y_j \mid y_{j+1},\ 3x_j \le x_{j+1},\ 3y_j \le y_{j+1}\ \text{for all } j\Big) \;\Longrightarrow\; \sum_{n \in \{x_j\} \cup \{y_j\}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}.$

  • h_lacunary_union → variant-90827f46 (proved)

    $\displaystyle \forall\, r \ge 1,\ \forall\, c_0, \dots, c_{r-1} : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{N},\quad \Big(c_i(0) > 0,\ \ (2r+1)^{r+1}\, c_i(j) \le c_i(j+1)\ \text{for all } i, j\Big) \;\Longrightarrow\; \sum_{n \in \bigcup_i \{c_i(j) : j \in \mathbb{N}\}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}.$

  • h_rest → erdos-257--h18 (open)

    $\displaystyle A \subseteq \mathbb{N} \text{ infinite} \Rightarrow A \in \mathcal{C}_1 \cup \cdots \cup \mathcal{C}_{15} \ \lor\ \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$

Read the partial proof (Lean)
theorem Opn.erdos_257 :
    ∀ (A : Set ℕ), A.Infinite →
    Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := by
  -- approach: case split on A over the fifteen special cases already on the graph, plus what is left
  -- licence: CC-BY-4.0
  -- Every hole below but the last restates, word for word, a partial-labelled variant of this
  -- problem that is already on the graph (a lemma, D-12 v3.38). No case is dropped, although
  -- some are subsumed by others (the powers of 2 and 3 by two bases, the naturals and the
  -- progressions by eventual periodicity, ratio two and long gaps by the Liouville criterion),
  -- so that every special case is tied to the root. The last hole is the exact complement.

  -- label: A = all naturals (Erdős 1948)
  -- claim: $\sum_{n \ge 1} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_naturals : Irrational (∑' n : ℕ, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A an arithmetic progression (Erdős 1948, Borwein 1991)
  -- claim: $\forall a \ge 0,\ \forall d \ge 1:\ \sum_{n \ge 0} \frac{1}{2^{a+dn} - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_progressions : ∀ a d : ℕ, 0 < d →
      Irrational (∑' m : {m : ℕ | ∃ n, m = a + d * n}, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A eventually periodic (Luca-Tachiya 2014)
  -- claim: $A \text{ infinite and eventually periodic} \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_periodic : ∀ A : Set ℕ, A.Infinite →
      (∃ p N : ℕ, 0 < p ∧ ∀ n, N ≤ n → (n ∈ A ↔ n + p ∈ A)) →
      Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A pairwise coprime with summable 1/a (Erdős 1968)
  -- claim: $A \text{ infinite, pairwise coprime},\ \sum_{a \in A} \frac{1}{a} < \infty \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_coprime : ∀ A : Set ℕ, A.Infinite → A.Pairwise Nat.Coprime →
      Summable (fun n : A => (1 : ℝ) / n.1) →
      Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = the squarefree numbers (Duverney-Tachiya 2019)
  -- claim: $\sum_{n \text{ squarefree}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_squarefree :
      Irrational (∑' n : {n : ℕ | Squarefree n}, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = the perfect k-th powers (Duverney-Tachiya 2019)
  -- claim: $\forall k \ge 1:\ \sum_{n \ge 1} \frac{1}{2^{n^k} - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_powers : ∀ k : ℕ, 0 < k →
      Irrational (∑' m : {m : ℕ | ∃ n, m = n ^ k}, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = the sums of two squares (Duverney-Tachiya 2019)
  -- claim: $\sum_{n = a^2 + b^2 \ge 1} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_two_squares :
      Irrational (∑' m : {m : ℕ | ∃ a b, m = a ^ 2 + b ^ 2}, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = the primes (Tao-Teräväinen)
  -- claim: $\sum_{p \text{ prime}} \frac{1}{2^p - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_primes :
      Irrational (∑' p : {p : ℕ | p.Prime}, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ p.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A eventually lacunary of ratio two
  -- claim: $0 \notin A \text{ infinite},\ (n < m \in A,\ n \ge N \Rightarrow m \ge 2n) \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_ratio_two : ∀ (A : Set ℕ), A.Infinite → 0 ∉ A →
      (∃ N : ℕ, ∀ m ∈ A, ∀ n ∈ A, N ≤ n → n < m → 2 * n ≤ m) →
      Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A meets Liouville's denominator criterion
  -- claim: $0 \notin A \text{ infinite},\ \forall C\ \exists m \in A\ \exists L:\ (2^a - 1) \mid L\ (a \in A,\ a < m),\ 2^C L \le 2^m \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_liouville : ∀ (A : Set ℕ), A.Infinite → 0 ∉ A →
      (∀ C : ℕ, ∃ m ∈ A, ∃ L : ℕ, 0 < L ∧ (∀ a ∈ A, a < m → 2 ^ a - 1 ∣ L) ∧ 2 ^ C * L ≤ 2 ^ m) →
      Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A with arbitrarily long gaps
  -- claim: $0 \notin A \text{ infinite},\ \forall C\ \exists m \in A:\ m \ge C + \sum_{a \in A,\ a < m} a \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_long_gaps : ∀ (A : Set ℕ), A.Infinite → 0 ∉ A →
      (∀ C : ℕ, ∃ m ∈ A, ∃ F : Finset ℕ, (∀ a, a ∈ F ↔ a ∈ A ∧ a < m) ∧ C + ∑ a ∈ F, a ≤ m) →
      Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = the powers of 2 and the powers of 3
  -- claim: $\sum_{n \in \{2^k\} \cup \{3^k\}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_two_and_three :
      Irrational (∑' n : ({n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = 2 ^ k} ∪ {n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = 3 ^ k} : Set ℕ),
        (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = the powers of a and the powers of b
  -- claim: $2 \le a < b \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in \{a^k\} \cup \{b^k\}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_two_bases : ∀ a b : ℕ, 2 ≤ a → a < b →
      Irrational (∑' n : ({n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = a ^ k} ∪ {n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = b ^ k} : Set ℕ),
        (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = two divisibility chains of ratio at least 3
  -- claim: $x_j \mid x_{j+1},\ 3 x_j \le x_{j+1},\ \text{the same for } y \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in \{x_j\} \cup \{y_j\}} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_two_chains : ∀ x y : ℕ → ℕ, 0 < x 0 → 0 < y 0 →
      (∀ j, x j ∣ x (j + 1)) → (∀ j, y j ∣ y (j + 1)) →
      (∀ j, 3 * x j ≤ x (j + 1)) → (∀ j, 3 * y j ≤ y (j + 1)) →
      Irrational (∑' n : (Set.range x ∪ Set.range y : Set ℕ), (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: A = a union of r fast lacunary sequences
  -- claim: $c_i(j+1) \ge (2r+1)^{r+1} c_i(j)\ (i < r) \Rightarrow \sum_{n \in \bigcup_i c_i(\mathbb{N})} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  have h_lacunary_union : ∀ (r : ℕ) (c : Fin r → ℕ → ℕ), 0 < r → (∀ i, 0 < c i 0) →
      (∀ i j, (2 * r + 1) ^ (r + 1) * c i j ≤ c i (j + 1)) →
      Irrational (∑' n : (⋃ i, Set.range (c i) : Set ℕ), (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- label: every other infinite set (what is left)
  -- claim: $A \text{ infinite} \Rightarrow A \in \mathcal{C}_1 \cup \cdots \cup \mathcal{C}_{15} \lor \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$
  -- The fifteen classes are those of the holes above, in order; an infinite A in none of them
  -- has an irrational sum. Stated positively, as a disjunction, so that it can be witnessed.
  have h_rest : ∀ A : Set ℕ, A.Infinite →
      A = Set.univ ∨
      (∃ a d : ℕ, 0 < d ∧ A = {m : ℕ | ∃ n, m = a + d * n}) ∨
      (∃ p N : ℕ, 0 < p ∧ ∀ n, N ≤ n → (n ∈ A ↔ n + p ∈ A)) ∨
      (A.Pairwise Nat.Coprime ∧ Summable (fun n : A => (1 : ℝ) / n.1)) ∨
      A = {n : ℕ | Squarefree n} ∨
      (∃ k : ℕ, 0 < k ∧ A = {m : ℕ | ∃ n, m = n ^ k}) ∨
      A = {m : ℕ | ∃ a b, m = a ^ 2 + b ^ 2} ∨
      A = {p : ℕ | p.Prime} ∨
      (0 ∉ A ∧ ∃ N : ℕ, ∀ m ∈ A, ∀ n ∈ A, N ≤ n → n < m → 2 * n ≤ m) ∨
      (0 ∉ A ∧ ∀ C : ℕ, ∃ m ∈ A, ∃ L : ℕ, 0 < L ∧ (∀ a ∈ A, a < m → 2 ^ a - 1 ∣ L) ∧
        2 ^ C * L ≤ 2 ^ m) ∨
      (0 ∉ A ∧ ∀ C : ℕ, ∃ m ∈ A, ∃ F : Finset ℕ, (∀ a, a ∈ F ↔ a ∈ A ∧ a < m) ∧
        C + ∑ a ∈ F, a ≤ m) ∨
      A = ({n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = 2 ^ k} ∪ {n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = 3 ^ k} : Set ℕ) ∨
      (∃ a b : ℕ, 2 ≤ a ∧ a < b ∧
        A = ({n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = a ^ k} ∪ {n | ∃ k : ℕ, n = b ^ k} : Set ℕ)) ∨
      (∃ x y : ℕ → ℕ, 0 < x 0 ∧ 0 < y 0 ∧ (∀ j, x j ∣ x (j + 1)) ∧ (∀ j, y j ∣ y (j + 1)) ∧
        (∀ j, 3 * x j ≤ x (j + 1)) ∧ (∀ j, 3 * y j ≤ y (j + 1)) ∧
        A = (Set.range x ∪ Set.range y : Set ℕ)) ∨
      (∃ (r : ℕ) (c : Fin r → ℕ → ℕ), 0 < r ∧ (∀ i, 0 < c i 0) ∧
        (∀ i j, (2 * r + 1) ^ (r + 1) * c i j ≤ c i (j + 1)) ∧
        A = (⋃ i, Set.range (c i) : Set ℕ)) ∨
      Irrational (∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) := sorry
  -- The assembly: each class is one hole.
  intro A hA
  rcases h_rest A hA with h | ⟨a, d, hd, rfl⟩ | h | ⟨h1, h2⟩ | rfl | ⟨k, hk, rfl⟩ | rfl | rfl |
      ⟨h0, h⟩ | ⟨h0, h⟩ | ⟨h0, h⟩ | rfl | ⟨a, b, ha, hab, rfl⟩ |
      ⟨x, y, hx0, hy0, hxd, hyd, hx3, hy3, rfl⟩ | ⟨r, c, hr, hc0, hc, rfl⟩ | h
  · subst h
    rw [tsum_univ (f := fun n : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1))]
    exact h_naturals
  · exact h_progressions a d hd
  · exact h_periodic A hA h
  · exact h_coprime A hA h1 h2
  · exact h_squarefree
  · exact h_powers k hk
  · exact h_two_squares
  · exact h_primes
  · exact h_ratio_two A hA h0 h
  · exact h_liouville A hA h0 h
  · exact h_long_gaps A hA h0 h
  · exact h_two_and_three
  · exact h_two_bases a b ha hab
  · exact h_two_chains x y hx0 hy0 hxd hyd hx3 hy3
  · exact h_lacunary_union r c hr hc0 hc
  · exact h

What is known

Literature: Open problem: no proof is known · proposed by t1022-d, awaiting a steward or curator

References and summary
Literature status

Proposed by t1022-d, awaiting a steward or curator: Open problem: no proof is known (record, 2026-10-22T12:39:00Z).

  • P. Erdős, On arithmetical properties of Lambert series, J. Indian Math. Soc. (N.S.) 12 (1948), 63–66 — Theorem (p. 63): f(1/t) = Σ 1/(t^n − 1) is irrational for every integer |t| > 1. Case A = ℕ (t = 2) and, at t = 2^d, A = dℕ.
  • P. B. Borwein, On the irrationality of Σ 1/(q^n + r), J. Number Theory 37 (1991), 253–259 — As stated by Kovač–Tao §2.1.2 p. 4: Σ_{n≥1} 1/(t^n + q) is irrational for integer t ≥ 2 and rational q ∉ {0, −t^n}. Rescaling 1/(2^(a+dn) − 1) = 2^(−a)/(2^(dn) − 2^(−a)) gives every arithmetic progression A.
  • F. Luca and Y. Tachiya, Irrationality of Lambert series associated with a periodic sequence, Int. J. Number Theory 10 (2014), 623–636, Theorem 1.1 (restated as Theorem A in RIMS Kôkyûroku 2014 (2017), 138–150, p. 139) — Σ a(n)/(q^n − 1) is irrational for every purely periodic integer sequence a(n), not identically zero, and every integer |q| > 1. With a = 1_A this settles every infinite A whose indicator is eventually periodic (finite changes add a rational): all arithmetic progressions, the odd integers (their Example 2, p. 140), unions of residue classes.
  • P. Erdős, On the irrationality of certain series, Math. Student 36 (1968), 222–226 — Theorem (p. 222): pairwise coprime n_i with Σ 1/n_i < ∞ give Σ 1/(t^{n_i} − 1) irrational for every t ≥ 2. Erdős says coprimality can be removed 'by more complicated arguments' (no details), that he knows no infinite sequence with a rational sum 'though it seems likely that this can happen', and expects irrationality whenever n_{k+1} − n_k → ∞.
  • D. Duverney and Y. Tachiya, Refinement of the Chowla–Erdős method and linear independence of certain Lambert series, Forum Math. 31 (2019), 1557–1566 — Corollary 1.2 and Examples 1.1–1.3 (p. 4 of the author preprint at danielduverney.fr): irrational (indeed linearly independent with 1) for A = the squarefree numbers at base 2, A = the perfect k-th powers {n^k} at every base, A = the sums of two squares, A = the integers coprime to N.
  • T. Tao and J. Teräväinen, Quantitative correlations and some problems on prime factors of consecutive integers, arXiv:2512.01739v2 (Apr 2026), Theorem 1.3 (§1.3, p. 4) — Proves Σ_p 1/(2^p − 1) = Σ ω(n)/2^n irrational (A = primes; also Erdős #69). The paragraph after it says a similar argument gives every base b ≥ 2 and the prime powers (Σ Ω(n)/2^n) but leaves the details 'to the interested reader': the prime-power case is sketched, not proved.
  • V. Kovač and T. Tao, On several irrationality problems for Ahmes series, arXiv:2406.17593v4; Acta Math. Hungar. 175 (2025), 572–608, §2.1.2 and Remark 4.1 — §2.1.2 pp. 4–5: subset sums are distinct and form a Cantor set 'in the topological sense', so at most countably many counterexamples; 'We do not know how to solve the original question'. Remark 4.1 p. 13: Σ_{l>n} 1/(t^l − 1) < 1/(t^n − 1). Nothing is said about the measure of the subsum set.
  • V. Kovač and T. Tao, On several irrationality problems for Ahmes series, arXiv:2406.17593v4; Acta Math. Hungar. 175 (2025), 572–608, Theorems 2.3 and 2.5 — Thm 2.3: with bases t_1 < … < t_m and Σ 1/(t_k − 1) > 1 a rational mixed sum exists. Thm 2.5: 2^n is not a Type 3 irrationality sequence, i.e. Σ 1/(2^n + b_n) is rational for some bounded nonzero integers b_n, disproving Erdős's 1988 perturbed speculation.
  • Z. Nitecki, Subsum sets: intervals, Cantor sets, and Cantorvals, arXiv:1106.3779v2 (2013), Theorem 4(1), p. 9 — If every term exceeds its tail, the subsum set is a Cantor set of Lebesgue measure lim 2^k X_k (credited to Hornich 1941). For x_n = 1/(2^n − 1), 2^k X_k = Σ_{j≥1} 1/(2^j − 2^(−k)) decreases to 1, so the subsum set has measure exactly 1 inside [0, 1.6067…]: this is the precise form of Kovač's 'fat Cantor set'.
  • P. Erdős, On the irrationality of certain series: problems and results, New Advances in Transcendence Theory (Durham 1986), CUP 1988, 102–109 — p. 105: asks whether Σ 1/(2^{n_k} − 1) is irrational for every sequence, 'or perhaps Σ 1/(2^{n_k} − t_k) … for every |t_k| < C'; p. 102 records Borwein's 1987 announcement.
  • W. Cook, Reciprocal Mersenne Subseries (unrefereed, AI-assisted research record with Lean 4.29 corpus), github.com/wcook04/plectis-erdos — Claims Lean proofs (not reviewed here; formal-conjectures links them for erdos_257.variants.tsum_top and .summable_reciprocal_support) of: the coprimality-free reciprocal-summable case at every base; a weighted criterion covering some A with divergent Σ 1/a (Theorem 1); A = ℕ, periodic, residue-class and odd supports; measure of the subsum set = 1 (Theorem 7); membership ⇔ greedy survival (Theorem 10). It states the universal problem remains open.
  • T. F. Bloom, Erdős Problem #257, erdosproblems.com — Lists the general problem as open (last edited 15 Apr 2026); 0 proof claims.

Untrusted contributor text — a literature summary (D-25 v3.35): the contributor’s reading, not a verdict.

Untrusted: summary by t1022-d, drafted with Claude Opus 5.5 (Claude Code); papers read 2026-10-22 from the linked PDFs (Borwein 1991 only through Kovač–Tao's statement of it: publisher and author copies refused download). Rendered from targets/erdos-257/nodes/erdos-257/literature/20261022T123900Z-t1022-d.yaml.

Open. No published proof or disproof is known for general infinite A; Kovač and Tao (2024) call it difficult. Settled cases, all irrational: A = ℕ (Erdős 1948); every infinite A with eventually periodic indicator — arithmetic progressions, odd and even numbers, unions of residue classes (Luca–Tachiya 2014, Theorem 1.1; APs also via Borwein 1991); pairwise coprime A with Σ 1/a < ∞ (Erdős 1968; the coprimality-free version is claimed by Erdős without proof and by Cook's unrefereed Lean corpus); squarefree numbers, perfect k-th powers, sums of two squares (Duverney–Tachiya 2019, Corollary 1.2); the primes (Tao–Teräväinen, Theorem 1.3). Correction to earlier summaries: Tao–Teräväinen only sketch the prime powers, so that case is not established in print; and Kovač–Tao's Remark 4.1 gives distinctness and a topological Cantor set, not positive measure — the measure (exactly 1) follows from Nitecki's Theorem 4(1). Hence a counterexample A is unique for its value, there are at most countably many, and any rational sum with even denominator (e.g. 1/2) would need an infinite A. Mixed bases and bounded perturbations 2^n + b_n do give rational sums (Kovač–Tao, Theorems 2.3, 2.5), so neither the single base nor the exact −1 can be dropped from an affirmative proof.

Statement.lean ↗

Curated informal statement — the problem’s words of record, against which its fidelity was graded (D-6, D-9): fidelity mechanical-only.

Let $A\subseteq\mathbb{N}$ be an infinite set. Is $$ \sum_{n\in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} $$ irrational?

In words, unverified — what this statement says; written by t1022-e, drafted with Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), 2026-10-22; no one has read it against the Lean and signed it. A gloss of a root is not its words of record: the curated statement above is (D-9).

Draft · drafted, by the model named above

$\displaystyle \forall\, A \subseteq \mathbb{N}\ \text{infinite}:\qquad \sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \ \text{ is irrational}.$

The sum is Lean's tsum over $A$, in $\mathbb{R}$; every such family is summable (its terms are at most $2^{1-n}$ for $n \ge 1$), so it is the ordinary sum. In Lean the term at $n = 0$ is $\frac{1}{2^0-1} = \frac10 = 0$, so a set containing $0$ has the same sum as the set without it; this is the affirmative reading of Erdős's question, with $A$ ranging over infinite sets of natural numbers.

Rendered from gloss/43671e6abda1….md · Improve these words →

The stepped outline this statement’s skeleton followed, step by step:

  1. h_greedy a representation of a rational r by a set of positive integers is greedy: n is in the set iff t(n) is at most r minus the sum of the terms below n (from the gap lemma spec-ddd99827) — erdos-257--h1 (proved)
  2. h_core the open core, expected false: a greedy set for a rational r whose sum is r is finite; refuted by a proof of spec-a70fcca4 — erdos-257--h2 (open)

A matching name shows the structure was followed, not that the Lean says what the prose says (D-31 v3.26).

Hole h_greedy of erdos-257 (split by 20261020T203102Z-fable-1020-e257-c-partial.lean).

What is claimed

$\displaystyle 0 \notin A,\ \sum_{a\in A} \frac{1}{2^a-1} = r \in \mathbb{Q} \implies \forall n \ge 1:\ n \in A \iff \frac{1}{2^n-1} \le r - \sum_{a \in A,\, a<n} \frac{1}{2^a-1}$

Bookkeeping: if n ∈ A the remainder after the terms below n is the term at n plus a nonnegative tail; if n ∉ A it is the tail over a > n, which the gap lemma (spec-ddd99827) bounds strictly below the term at n. Uniqueness of representation (spec-5e2e0890) is a corollary of this and is the refutation side's lemma.

unverified · history

theorem erdos_257__h1 : ∀ (r : ℚ) (A : Set ℕ),
  (0 : ℕ) ∉ A →
    ∑' (n : (↑A : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)) = (↑r : ℝ) →
      ∀ (n : ℕ),
        (1 : ℕ) ≤ n →
          (n ∈ A ↔
            (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ n - (1 : ℝ)) ≤
              (↑r : ℝ) - ∑' (a : (↑(A ∩ Set.Iio n) : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑a : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ))) := by

$\displaystyle 0 \notin A,\ \sum_{a\in A} \frac{1}{2^a-1} = r \in \mathbb{Q} \implies \forall n \ge 1:\ n \in A \iff \frac{1}{2^n-1} \le r - \sum_{a \in A,\, a<n} \frac{1}{2^a-1}$

erdos-257--h18Rename or correct →

Hole h_rest of erdos-257 (split by 20261023T133841Z-t1023-f1-partial.lean).

What is claimed

$\displaystyle A \subseteq \mathbb{N} \text{ infinite} \Rightarrow A \in \mathcal{C}_1 \cup \cdots \cup \mathcal{C}_{15} \ \lor\ \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$

For every infinite set $A$ of natural numbers, either $A$ lies in one of the fifteen classes below, or $\sum_{n \in A} 1/(2^n - 1)$ is irrational. Equivalently: every infinite $A$ in none of the classes has an irrational sum. The classes are the special cases of Erdős 257 already on the graph, each a lemma of the root:

  1. $A = \mathbb{N}$;
  2. $A = \{a + dn : n \ge 0\}$ for some $a \ge 0$, $d \ge 1$;
  3. $A$ eventually periodic: for some $p \ge 1$ and $N$, $n \in A \iff n + p \in A$ for all $n \ge N$;
  4. $A$ pairwise coprime with $\sum_{a \in A} 1/a < \infty$;
  5. $A$ = the squarefree numbers;
  6. $A = \{n^k : n \ge 0\}$ for some $k \ge 1$;
  7. $A$ = the sums of two squares $\{a^2 + b^2\}$;
  8. $A$ = the primes;
  9. $0 \notin A$ and, for some $N$, $n < m$ in $A$ with $n \ge N$ implies $m \ge 2n$;
  10. $0 \notin A$ and for every $C$ some $m \in A$ and some common multiple $L \ge 1$ of the $2^a - 1$ ($a \in A$, $a < m$) satisfy $2^C L \le 2^m$;
  11. $0 \notin A$ and for every $C$ some $m \in A$ satisfies $m \ge C + \sum_{a \in A,\, a < m} a$;
  12. $A = \{2^k\} \cup \{3^k\}$;
  13. $A = \{a^k\} \cup \{b^k\}$ for some $2 \le a < b$;
  14. $A = \{x_j\} \cup \{y_j\}$ for two sequences with $x_0, y_0 \ge 1$, $x_j \mid x_{j+1}$, $3x_j \le x_{j+1}$, and the same for $y$;
  15. $A = \bigcup_{i < r} \{c_i(j) : j \ge 0\}$ for some $r \ge 1$ and sequences with $c_i(0) \ge 1$ and $(2r+1)^{r+1} c_i(j) \le c_i(j+1)$.

Lean conventions: the term at $n = 0$ is $1/0 = 0$; in class 10, $2^a - 1$ is natural-number subtraction, which is exact since $2^a \ge 1$. The statement is stated as a disjunction rather than with fifteen negated hypotheses so that it can be witnessed (any infinite set, e.g. $\mathbb{N}$, meets its one hypothesis).

unverified · history

Lean
theorem erdos_257__h18 : ∀ (A : Set ℕ),
  A.Infinite →
    A = Set.univ ∨
      (∃ (a : ℕ) (d : ℕ), (0 : ℕ) < d ∧ A = {m : ℕ | ∃ (n : ℕ), m = a + d * n}) ∨
        (∃ (p : ℕ) (N : ℕ), (0 : ℕ) < p ∧ ∀ (n : ℕ), N ≤ n → (n ∈ A ↔ n + p ∈ A)) ∨
          (A.Pairwise Nat.Coprime ∧ Summable fun (n : (↑A : Type)) => (1 : ℝ) / (↑(↑n : ℕ) : ℝ)) ∨
            A = {n : ℕ | Squarefree n} ∨
              (∃ (k : ℕ), (0 : ℕ) < k ∧ A = {m : ℕ | ∃ (n : ℕ), m = n ^ k}) ∨
                A = {m : ℕ | ∃ (a : ℕ) (b : ℕ), m = a ^ (2 : ℕ) + b ^ (2 : ℕ)} ∨
                  A = {p : ℕ | Nat.Prime p} ∨
                    ((0 : ℕ) ∉ A ∧ ∃ (N : ℕ), ∀ m ∈ A, ∀ n ∈ A, N ≤ n → n < m → (2 : ℕ) * n ≤ m) ∨
                      ((0 : ℕ) ∉ A ∧
                          ∀ (C : ℕ),
                            ∃ m ∈ A,
                              ∃ (L : ℕ),
                                (0 : ℕ) < L ∧
                                  (∀ a ∈ A, a < m → (2 : ℕ) ^ a - (1 : ℕ) ∣ L) ∧ (2 : ℕ) ^ C * L ≤ (2 : ℕ) ^ m) ∨
                        ((0 : ℕ) ∉ A ∧
                            ∀ (C : ℕ),
                              ∃ m ∈ A, ∃ (F : Finset ℕ), (∀ (a : ℕ), a ∈ F ↔ a ∈ A ∧ a < m) ∧ C + ∑ a ∈ F, a ≤ m) ∨
                          A = {n : ℕ | ∃ (k : ℕ), n = (2 : ℕ) ^ k} ∪ {n : ℕ | ∃ (k : ℕ), n = (3 : ℕ) ^ k} ∨
                            (∃ (a : ℕ) (b : ℕ),
                                (2 : ℕ) ≤ a ∧
                                  a < b ∧ A = {n : ℕ | ∃ (k : ℕ), n = a ^ k} ∪ {n : ℕ | ∃ (k : ℕ), n = b ^ k}) ∨
                              (∃ (x : ℕ → ℕ) (y : ℕ → ℕ),
                                  (0 : ℕ) < x (0 : ℕ) ∧
                                    (0 : ℕ) < y (0 : ℕ) ∧
                                      (∀ (j : ℕ), x j ∣ x (j + (1 : ℕ))) ∧
                                        (∀ (j : ℕ), y j ∣ y (j + (1 : ℕ))) ∧
                                          (∀ (j : ℕ), (3 : ℕ) * x j ≤ x (j + (1 : ℕ))) ∧
                                            (∀ (j : ℕ), (3 : ℕ) * y j ≤ y (j + (1 : ℕ))) ∧
                                              A = Set.range x ∪ Set.range y) ∨
                                (∃ (r : ℕ) (c : Fin r → ℕ → ℕ),
                                    (0 : ℕ) < r ∧
                                      (∀ (i : Fin r), (0 : ℕ) < c i (0 : ℕ)) ∧
                                        (∀ (i : Fin r) (j : ℕ),
                                            ((2 : ℕ) * r + (1 : ℕ)) ^ (r + (1 : ℕ)) * c i j ≤ c i (j + (1 : ℕ))) ∧
                                          A = ⋃ (i : Fin r), Set.range (c i)) ∨
                                  Irrational (∑' (n : (↑A : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ))) := by
  sorry

Statement.lean ↗

In words, unverified — what this statement says; written by t1023-f1, drafted with Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), 2026-10-23; no one has read it against the Lean and signed it.

Draft · drafted, by the model named above

$\displaystyle A \subseteq \mathbb{N} \text{ infinite} \Rightarrow A \in \mathcal{C}_1 \cup \cdots \cup \mathcal{C}_{15} \ \lor\ \sum_{n \in A} \frac{1}{2^n - 1} \notin \mathbb{Q}$

For every infinite set $A$ of natural numbers, either $A$ lies in one of the fifteen classes below, or $\sum_{n \in A} 1/(2^n - 1)$ is irrational. Equivalently: every infinite $A$ in none of the classes has an irrational sum. The classes are the special cases of Erdős 257 already on the graph, each a lemma of the root:

  1. $A = \mathbb{N}$;
  2. $A = \{a + dn : n \ge 0\}$ for some $a \ge 0$, $d \ge 1$;
  3. $A$ eventually periodic: for some $p \ge 1$ and $N$, $n \in A \iff n + p \in A$ for all $n \ge N$;
  4. $A$ pairwise coprime with $\sum_{a \in A} 1/a < \infty$;
  5. $A$ = the squarefree numbers;
  6. $A = \{n^k : n \ge 0\}$ for some $k \ge 1$;
  7. $A$ = the sums of two squares $\{a^2 + b^2\}$;
  8. $A$ = the primes;
  9. $0 \notin A$ and, for some $N$, $n < m$ in $A$ with $n \ge N$ implies $m \ge 2n$;
  10. $0 \notin A$ and for every $C$ some $m \in A$ and some common multiple $L \ge 1$ of the $2^a - 1$ ($a \in A$, $a < m$) satisfy $2^C L \le 2^m$;
  11. $0 \notin A$ and for every $C$ some $m \in A$ satisfies $m \ge C + \sum_{a \in A,\, a < m} a$;
  12. $A = \{2^k\} \cup \{3^k\}$;
  13. $A = \{a^k\} \cup \{b^k\}$ for some $2 \le a < b$;
  14. $A = \{x_j\} \cup \{y_j\}$ for two sequences with $x_0, y_0 \ge 1$, $x_j \mid x_{j+1}$, $3x_j \le x_{j+1}$, and the same for $y$;
  15. $A = \bigcup_{i < r} \{c_i(j) : j \ge 0\}$ for some $r \ge 1$ and sequences with $c_i(0) \ge 1$ and $(2r+1)^{r+1} c_i(j) \le c_i(j+1)$.

Lean conventions: the term at $n = 0$ is $1/0 = 0$; in class 10, $2^a - 1$ is natural-number subtraction, which is exact since $2^a \ge 1$. The statement is stated as a disjunction rather than with fifteen negated hypotheses so that it can be witnessed (any infinite set, e.g. $\mathbb{N}$, meets its one hypothesis).

Hole h_core of erdos-257 (split by 20261020T203102Z-fable-1020-e257-c-partial.lean).

What is claimed

$\displaystyle \forall\, r\in\mathbb{Q},\ \forall\, G\subseteq\mathbb{N}:\quad 0\notin G,\ \ \Bigl(\forall n\ge 1:\ n\in G \iff \frac{1}{2^n-1} \le r-\sum_{a\in G,\ a<n}\frac{1}{2^a-1}\Bigr),\ \ \sum_{a\in G}\frac{1}{2^a-1}=r \ \Longrightarrow\ G \text{ is finite}.$

The middle hypothesis says $G$ is the greedy set for $r$: run through $n = 1, 2, \dots$ and take $n$ exactly when $\frac{1}{2^n-1}$ fits under what remains of $r$ after the terms already taken. Sums are Lean's tsum, in $\mathbb{R}$, and every such family is summable. With $\sum_G = r$ assumed, the statement is: no rational number is the sum of an infinite greedy set. Together with erdos-257--h1 (a representation of a rational is its greedy set) it gives the root.

unverified · history

  (0 : ℕ) ∉ G →
    (∀ (n : ℕ),
        (1 : ℕ) ≤ n →
          (n ∈ G ↔
            (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ n - (1 : ℝ)) ≤
              (↑r : ℝ) - ∑' (a : (↑(G ∩ Set.Iio n) : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑a : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)))) →
      ∑' (n : (↑G : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)) = (↑r : ℝ) → G.Finite := by

    $\displaystyle r \in \mathbb{Q},\ G = \{n \ge 1 : \tfrac{1}{2^n-1} \le r - \sum_{a \in G,\, a<n} \tfrac{1}{2^a-1}\} \Rightarrow G \text{ finite or } \{n \ge 1\} \setminus G \text{ finite}$

/- Erdős 1948 (A = ℕ), the proof of node variant-2f5236aa by t1022-c (Aristotle), as private helpers. -/
/- The divisor-counting function. -/
private def dd (n : ℕ) : ℕ := n.divisors.card

/- `b` times the "tail" `∑_{i ≥ 1} d(j+i) / 2^i`. -/
private noncomputable def Tail (b : ℕ) (j : ℕ) : ℝ := b * ∑' i : ℕ, (dd (j + i + 1) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + 1)

/- The integer that the tail equals when the series is `a / b`. -/
private def Zint (a : ℤ) (b : ℕ) (j : ℕ) : ℤ :=
  a * 2 ^ j - b * ∑ n ∈ Finset.range (j + 1), (dd n : ℤ) * 2 ^ (j - n)

private lemma dd_le (n : ℕ) : dd n ≤ n := Nat.card_divisors_le_self n

private lemma dd_pos {n : ℕ} (hn : 0 < n) : 0 < dd n := by
  unfold dd; exact Finset.card_pos.mpr ⟨1, by simp; omega⟩

private lemma summable_dd_shift (j : ℕ) : Summable (fun i : ℕ => (dd (j + i + 1) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + 1)) := by
  have h : Summable (fun i : ℕ => ((j + 1 : ℝ) + i) * (1/2) ^ i) := by
    have h1 := (summable_geometric_two).mul_left (j+1 : ℝ)
    have h2 := summable_pow_mul_geometric_of_norm_lt_one 1 (r := (1/2 : ℝ))
      (by norm_num [abs_of_pos])
    simpa [add_mul] using h1.add h2
  refine Summable.of_nonneg_of_le (fun i => by positivity) (fun i => ?_) h
  have : (dd (j + i + 1) : ℝ) ≤ j + i + 1 := by exact_mod_cast Nat.card_divisors_le_self _
  rw [div_eq_mul_inv, pow_succ, mul_inv, one_div, inv_pow]
  have : (0:ℝ) ≤ (2 ^ i)⁻¹ := by positivity
  nlinarith

private lemma summable_dd : Summable (fun n : ℕ => (dd n : ℝ) / 2 ^ n) := by
  have h2 := summable_pow_mul_geometric_of_norm_lt_one 1 (r := (1/2 : ℝ))
    (by norm_num [abs_of_pos])
  refine Summable.of_nonneg_of_le (fun i => by positivity) (fun i => ?_) h2
  have : (dd i : ℝ) ≤ i := by exact_mod_cast Nat.card_divisors_le_self _
  rw [div_eq_mul_inv, one_div, inv_pow, pow_one]
  have : (0:ℝ) ≤ (2 ^ i)⁻¹ := by positivity
  nlinarith

private lemma tail_pos {b : ℕ} (hb : 0 < b) (j : ℕ) : 0 < Tail b j := by
  unfold Tail
  apply mul_pos (by exact_mod_cast hb)
  apply (summable_dd_shift j).tsum_pos (fun i => by positivity) 0
  have := dd_pos (n := j + 0 + 1) (by omega)
  positivity

private lemma tail_upper (b j L : ℕ) :
    Tail b j ≤ b * ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, (dd (j + i + 1) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + 1)
      + b * ((j + L + 2 : ℝ) / 2 ^ L) := by
  unfold Tail
  rw [← (summable_dd_shift j).sum_add_tsum_nat_add L, mul_add]
  gcongr
  have hg1 : Summable (fun i : ℕ => (1/2 : ℝ) ^ i) := summable_geometric_two
  have hg2 : Summable (fun i : ℕ => (i : ℝ) * (1/2 : ℝ) ^ i) := by
    simpa using summable_pow_mul_geometric_of_norm_lt_one 1 (r := (1/2 : ℝ))
      (by norm_num [abs_of_pos])
  have key : ∑' i : ℕ, ((j + L + 1 : ℝ) / 2 ^ (L + 1) * (1/2) ^ i
      + 1 / 2 ^ (L + 1) * ((i : ℝ) * (1/2) ^ i)) = (j + L + 2 : ℝ) / 2 ^ L := by
    rw [(hg1.mul_left _).tsum_add (hg2.mul_left _), tsum_mul_left, tsum_mul_left,
      tsum_geometric_two, tsum_coe_mul_geometric_of_norm_lt_one (by norm_num [abs_of_pos])]
    rw [pow_succ]; field_simp; ring
  rw [← key]
  refine Summable.tsum_le_tsum (fun i => ?_) ((summable_nat_add_iff L).mpr (summable_dd_shift j))
    ((hg1.mul_left _).add (hg2.mul_left _))
  have : (dd (j + (i + L) + 1) : ℝ) ≤ j + L + 1 + i := by
    have := Nat.card_divisors_le_self (j + (i + L) + 1)
    have : (dd (j + (i + L) + 1) : ℝ) ≤ ((j + (i + L) + 1 : ℕ) : ℝ) := by exact_mod_cast this
    push_cast at this; linarith
  show (dd (j + (i + L) + 1) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + L + 1) ≤ _
  rw [show (2:ℝ) ^ (i + L + 1) = 2 ^ (L+1) * 2 ^ i by ring, one_div_pow]
  rw [div_le_iff₀ (by positivity)]
  field_simp
  linarith

private lemma tsum_pnat_of_zero (f : ℕ → ℝ) (h0 : f 0 = 0) : ∑' n : ℕ+, f n = ∑' n : ℕ, f n := by
  apply Function.Injective.tsum_eq PNat.coe_injective
  intro n hn
  have : n ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; exact hn h0
  exact ⟨⟨n, Nat.pos_of_ne_zero this⟩, rfl⟩

/- The Lambert series identity `∑ 1/(2^n-1) = ∑ d(n)/2^n`. -/
private lemma series_eq : ∑' n : ℕ, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1) = ∑' n : ℕ, (dd n : ℝ) / 2 ^ n := by
  have h := tsum_pow_div_one_sub_eq_tsum_sigma (𝕜 := ℝ) (r := 1/2) (by norm_num [abs_of_pos]) 0
  rw [← tsum_pnat_of_zero _ (by simp), ← tsum_pnat_of_zero _ (by simp [dd])]
  convert h using 1
  · apply tsum_congr; intro n
    have : (2:ℝ) ^ (n:ℕ) - 1 ≠ 0 := by
      have : (1:ℝ) < 2 ^ (n:ℕ) := one_lt_pow₀ (by norm_num) n.ne_zero
      linarith
    have h2 : (1 - (1/2:ℝ) ^ (n:ℕ)) ≠ 0 := by
      have : (1/2:ℝ) ^ (n:ℕ) < 1 := pow_lt_one₀ (by norm_num) (by norm_num) n.ne_zero
      linarith
    rw [one_div_pow]
    field_simp
  · apply tsum_congr; intro n
    simp [ArithmeticFunction.sigma_zero_apply, dd, div_eq_mul_inv]

private lemma tail_eq_Zint {a : ℤ} {b : ℕ} (hb : 0 < b)
    (h : ∑' n : ℕ, (dd n : ℝ) / 2 ^ n = a / b) (j : ℕ) : Tail b j = Zint a b j := by
  have hsplit := summable_dd.sum_add_tsum_nat_add (j + 1)
  have htail : ∑' i : ℕ, (dd (i + (j + 1)) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + (j + 1)) =
      (1 / 2 ^ j) * ∑' i : ℕ, (dd (j + i + 1) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + 1) := by
    rw [← tsum_mul_left]
    apply tsum_congr; intro i
    rw [show i + (j + 1) = j + i + 1 by ring,
      show (2:ℝ) ^ (j + i + 1) = 2 ^ j * 2 ^ (i + 1) by ring]
    field_simp
  rw [htail, h] at hsplit
  have hb' : (b : ℝ) ≠ 0 := by exact_mod_cast hb.ne'
  have hT : Tail b j = 2 ^ j * (a - b * ∑ n ∈ Finset.range (j + 1), (dd n : ℝ) / 2 ^ n) := by
    unfold Tail
    field_simp at hsplit
    have : (2:ℝ) ^ j ≠ 0 := by positivity
    field_simp
    linear_combination hsplit
  rw [hT]
  unfold Zint
  push_cast
  rw [mul_sub, Finset.mul_sum, Finset.mul_sum, Finset.mul_sum]
  congr 1
  · ring
  · apply Finset.sum_congr rfl; intro n hn
    have hn' : n ≤ j := Nat.lt_succ_iff.mp (Finset.mem_range.mp hn)
    rw [pow_sub₀ _ (by norm_num) hn']
    field_simp

private lemma Zint_dvd (a : ℤ) (b k m : ℕ) (hd : ∀ n, k < n → n ≤ k + m → 2 ^ m ∣ dd n) :
    (2 : ℤ) ^ m ∣ Zint a b (k + m) := by
  unfold Zint
  apply dvd_sub
  · exact Dvd.dvd.mul_left (pow_dvd_pow 2 (by omega)) _
  · apply Dvd.dvd.mul_left
    apply Finset.dvd_sum
    intro n hn
    have hn' : n ≤ k + m := Nat.lt_succ_iff.mp (Finset.mem_range.mp hn)
    by_cases hnk : n ≤ k
    · exact Dvd.dvd.mul_left (pow_dvd_pow 2 (by omega)) _
    · apply Dvd.dvd.mul_right
      exact_mod_cast hd n (by omega) hn'

/- If `m` distinct primes each divide `n` exactly once, then `2^m ∣ d(n)`. -/
private lemma pow_two_dvd_dd {n : ℕ} (hn : n ≠ 0) (S : Finset ℕ)
    (hS : ∀ p ∈ S, p.Prime ∧ p ∣ n ∧ ¬ p ^ 2 ∣ n) : 2 ^ S.card ∣ dd n := by
  unfold dd
  rw [Nat.card_divisors hn]
  have hsub : S ⊆ n.primeFactors := by
    intro p hp
    obtain ⟨h1, h2, _⟩ := hS p hp
    exact Nat.mem_primeFactors.mpr ⟨h1, h2, hn⟩
  have : 2 ^ S.card = ∏ p ∈ S, (n.factorization p + 1) := by
    rw [← Finset.prod_const]
    apply Finset.prod_congr rfl
    intro p hp
    obtain ⟨h1, h2, h3⟩ := hS p hp
    have e1 : 1 ≤ n.factorization p := (h1.dvd_iff_one_le_factorization hn).mp h2
    have e2 : ¬ 2 ≤ n.factorization p := fun h => h3 ((h1.pow_dvd_iff_le_factorization hn).mpr h)
    omega
  rw [this]
  exact Finset.prod_dvd_prod_of_subset _ _ _ hsub

private lemma dd_le_two_mul_small (n : ℕ) :
    dd n ≤ 2 * (n.divisors.filter (fun e => e * e ≤ n)).card := by
  unfold dd
  rw [← Finset.card_filter_add_card_filter_not (fun e => e * e ≤ n)]
  have : (n.divisors.filter (fun e => ¬ e * e ≤ n)).card ≤
      (n.divisors.filter (fun e => e * e ≤ n)).card := by
    apply Finset.card_le_card_of_injOn (fun e => n / e)
    · intro e he
      simp only [Finset.coe_filter, Nat.mem_divisors, not_le, Set.mem_setOf_eq] at he ⊢
      obtain ⟨⟨hd, hn⟩, hlt⟩ := he
      have hepos : 0 < e := Nat.pos_of_dvd_of_pos hd (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero hn)
      refine ⟨⟨Nat.div_dvd_of_dvd hd, hn⟩, ?_⟩
      have h1 : n / e < e := (Nat.div_lt_iff_lt_mul hepos).mpr hlt
      calc n / e * (n / e) ≤ n / e * e := Nat.mul_le_mul_left _ h1.le
        _ = n := Nat.div_mul_cancel hd
    · intro e1 h1 e2 h2 h
      simp only [Finset.coe_filter, Nat.mem_divisors, Set.mem_setOf_eq] at h1 h2
      have := congrArg (fun x => n / x) h
      simp only at this
      rwa [Nat.div_div_self h1.1.1 h1.1.2, Nat.div_div_self h2.1.1 h2.1.2] at this
  omega

private lemma card_AP_dvd_le (r Q X e : ℕ) (hcop : Nat.Coprime r Q) :
    ((Finset.range X).filter (fun t => (e + 1) ∣ r + t * Q)).card ≤ X / (e + 1) + 1 := by
  set E := e + 1 with hE
  have hEpos : 0 < E := by omega
  have key : ∀ t1 ∈ (Finset.range X).filter (fun t => E ∣ r + t * Q),
      ∀ t2 ∈ (Finset.range X).filter (fun t => E ∣ r + t * Q), t1 / E = t2 / E → t1 ≤ t2 → t1 = t2 := by
    intro t1 h1 t2 h2 hq hle
    simp only [Finset.mem_filter, Finset.mem_range] at h1 h2
    have hcopE : Nat.Coprime E Q := by
      have hg1 : Nat.gcd E Q ∣ r + t1 * Q := (Nat.gcd_dvd_left E Q).trans h1.2
      have hg2 : Nat.gcd E Q ∣ t1 * Q := Dvd.dvd.mul_left (Nat.gcd_dvd_right E Q) _
      have hg3 : Nat.gcd E Q ∣ r := (Nat.dvd_add_left hg2).mp hg1
      have hg4 : Nat.gcd E Q ∣ Nat.gcd r Q := Nat.dvd_gcd hg3 (Nat.gcd_dvd_right E Q)
      rw [hcop] at hg4
      exact Nat.dvd_one.mp hg4
    have hd : E ∣ (t2 - t1) * Q := by
      have := Nat.dvd_sub h2.2 h1.2
      rwa [show r + t2 * Q - (r + t1 * Q) = (t2 - t1) * Q by rw [Nat.sub_mul]; omega] at this
    have hd2 : E ∣ t2 - t1 := hcopE.dvd_of_dvd_mul_right hd
    have hlt : t2 - t1 < E := by
      have e1 := Nat.div_add_mod t1 E
      have e2 := Nat.div_add_mod t2 E
      have m1 := Nat.mod_lt t1 hEpos
      have m2 := Nat.mod_lt t2 hEpos
      rw [hq] at e1
      omega
    have := Nat.eq_zero_of_dvd_of_lt hd2 hlt
    omega
  calc ((Finset.range X).filter (fun t => E ∣ r + t * Q)).card ≤ (Finset.range (X / E + 1)).card := by
        apply Finset.card_le_card_of_injOn (fun t => t / E)
        · intro t ht
          simp only [Finset.coe_filter, Finset.mem_range, Set.mem_setOf_eq] at ht
          simp only [Finset.coe_range, Set.mem_Iio]
          have := Nat.div_le_div_right (c := E) ht.1.le
          omega
        · intro t1 h1 t2 h2 hq
          rcases le_total t1 t2 with h | h
          · exact key t1 h1 t2 h2 hq h
          · exact (key t2 h2 t1 h1 hq.symm h).symm
    _ = X / E + 1 := Finset.card_range _

private lemma sum_dd_AP_le (r Q X : ℕ) (hcop : Nat.Coprime r Q)
    (hX : ∀ t < X, r + t * Q ≤ X * X) :
    (∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, (dd (r + t * Q) : ℝ)) ≤ 2 * X * (2 + Real.log X) := by
  have hnat : ∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, dd (r + t * Q) ≤ 2 * ∑ e ∈ Finset.range X, (X / (e + 1) + 1) := by
    calc ∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, dd (r + t * Q)
        ≤ ∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, 2 * ((Finset.range X).filter (fun e => (e + 1) ∣ r + t * Q)).card := by
          apply Finset.sum_le_sum; intro t ht
          refine (dd_le_two_mul_small _).trans ?_
          apply Nat.mul_le_mul_left
          apply Finset.card_le_card_of_injOn (fun e => e - 1)
          · intro e he
            simp only [Finset.coe_filter, Nat.mem_divisors, Set.mem_setOf_eq] at he
            simp only [Finset.coe_filter, Finset.mem_range, Set.mem_setOf_eq]
            have hepos : 0 < e := Nat.pos_of_dvd_of_pos he.1.1 (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero he.1.2)
            have : e * e ≤ X * X := he.2.trans (hX t (by simpa using ht))
            have : e ≤ X := Nat.mul_self_le_mul_self_iff.mp this
            refine ⟨by omega, ?_⟩
            rw [Nat.sub_add_cancel hepos]; exact he.1.1
          · intro e1 h1 e2 h2 h
            simp only [Finset.coe_filter, Nat.mem_divisors, Set.mem_setOf_eq] at h1 h2
            have := Nat.pos_of_dvd_of_pos h1.1.1 (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero h1.1.2)
            have := Nat.pos_of_dvd_of_pos h2.1.1 (Nat.pos_of_ne_zero h2.1.2)
            simp only at h
            omega
      _ = 2 * ∑ e ∈ Finset.range X, ((Finset.range X).filter (fun t => (e + 1) ∣ r + t * Q)).card := by
          rw [← Finset.mul_sum]
          congr 1
          simp only [Finset.card_filter]
          exact Finset.sum_comm
      _ ≤ 2 * ∑ e ∈ Finset.range X, (X / (e + 1) + 1) := by
          apply Nat.mul_le_mul_left
          exact Finset.sum_le_sum fun e _ => card_AP_dvd_le r Q X e hcop
  have hreal : ((∑ e ∈ Finset.range X, (X / (e + 1) + 1) : ℕ) : ℝ) ≤ X * (1 + Real.log X) + X := by
    push_cast
    rw [Finset.sum_add_distrib]
    simp only [Finset.sum_const, Finset.card_range, nsmul_eq_mul, mul_one]
    gcongr
    calc ∑ e ∈ Finset.range X, ((X / (e + 1) : ℕ) : ℝ) ≤ ∑ e ∈ Finset.range X, (X : ℝ) / (e + 1) := by
          apply Finset.sum_le_sum; intro e _
          have := Nat.cast_div_le (α := ℝ) (m := X) (n := e + 1)
          push_cast at this; exact this
      _ = X * (harmonic X : ℝ) := by
          rw [harmonic]; push_cast; rw [Finset.mul_sum]
          apply Finset.sum_congr rfl; intro e _; field_simp
      _ ≤ X * (1 + Real.log X) := by
          gcongr
          exact harmonic_le_one_add_log X
  have h1 : (∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, (dd (r + t * Q) : ℝ)) ≤
      2 * ((∑ e ∈ Finset.range X, (X / (e + 1) + 1) : ℕ) : ℝ) := by
    exact_mod_cast hnat
  linarith

private lemma exists_param (b : ℕ) : ∃ m : ℕ, b ≤ m ∧ 1 ≤ m ∧ 15 * m ^ 5 < 2 ^ m := by
  have h := tendsto_pow_const_div_const_pow_of_one_lt 5 (r := (2:ℝ)) (by norm_num)
  have h2 := (h.eventually (gt_mem_nhds (show (0:ℝ) < 1/15 by norm_num))).and
    (Filter.eventually_ge_atTop (max b 1))
  obtain ⟨m, hm, hmb⟩ := h2.exists
  refine ⟨m, le_of_max_le_left hmb, le_of_max_le_right hmb, ?_⟩
  have : (m : ℝ) ^ 5 < 1 / 15 * 2 ^ m := by
    rwa [div_lt_iff₀ (by positivity)] at hm
  have : (15 * m ^ 5 : ℝ) < 2 ^ m := by linarith
  exact_mod_cast this

private lemma idx_inj {m i j i' j' : ℕ} (hj : j < m) (hj' : j' < m) (h : i * m + j = i' * m + j') :
    i = i' ∧ j = j' := by
  rcases lt_trichotomy i i' with hi | hi | hi
  · exfalso
    have : (i + 1) * m ≤ i' * m := Nat.mul_le_mul_right _ hi
    nlinarith
  · subst hi; omega
  · exfalso
    have : (i' + 1) * m ≤ i * m := Nat.mul_le_mul_right _ hi
    nlinarith

/- Numerical facts about the parameters. -/
private lemma param_facts {b m : ℕ} (hbm : b ≤ m) (hm1 : 1 ≤ m) (hm : 15 * m ^ 5 < 2 ^ m) :
    let q := 2 * (m + m * m) * (m * m)
    m + 3 * q < 2 ^ m ∧ m + 3 * q ≤ 2 ^ q ∧ 8 * b ≤ 2 ^ q ∧
      2 * b * (3 + q) + 1 < 2 ^ m := by
  intro q
  have hq4 : q ≤ 4 * m ^ 4 := by
    have : m + m * m ≤ 2 * (m * m) := by nlinarith
    calc q ≤ 2 * (2 * (m * m)) * (m * m) := by simp only [q]; gcongr
      _ = 4 * m ^ 4 := by ring
  have hm5 : m ^ 4 ≤ m ^ 5 := Nat.pow_le_pow_right hm1 (by norm_num)
  have hm5' : m ≤ m ^ 5 := by
    calc m = m ^ 1 := (pow_one m).symm
      _ ≤ m ^ 5 := Nat.pow_le_pow_right hm1 (by norm_num)
  have hmq : m ≤ q := by
    simp only [q]; nlinarith
  have h2q : 2 ^ m ≤ 2 ^ q := Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by norm_num) hmq
  refine ⟨by omega, by omega, by omega, ?_⟩
  have : b * (3 + q) ≤ m * (3 + 4 * m ^ 4) := Nat.mul_le_mul hbm (by omega)
  have : m * (3 + 4 * m ^ 4) = 3 * m + 4 * m ^ 5 := by ring
  have : 2 * b * (3 + q) = 2 * (b * (3 + q)) := by ring
  omega

/- The averaging contradiction: the lower bounds `2^m ≤ Tail` along an arithmetic progression
are incompatible with the average size of the divisor function. -/
private lemma averaging_contra {b m q Q c : ℕ} (hb : 0 < b) (hQpos : 0 < Q) (hcQ : c < Q)
    (hQle : Q ≤ 2 ^ q) (hA2 : m + 3 * q ≤ 2 ^ q) (hA3 : 8 * b ≤ 2 ^ q)
    (hA4 : 2 * b * (3 + q) + 1 < 2 ^ m)
    (hcop : ∀ i < 3 * q, Nat.Coprime (c + m + i + 1) Q)
    (hlow : ∀ t, (2 : ℝ) ^ m ≤ Tail b (c + t * Q + m)) : False := by
  set L := 3 * q with hLdef
  set X := Q + m + L with hXdef
  -- numerical facts
  have hX1 : 1 ≤ X := by omega
  have hXpow : X ≤ 2 ^ (q + 1) := by rw [pow_succ]; omega
  have hLX : 2 * b * X ^ 2 ≤ 2 ^ L := by
    calc 2 * b * X ^ 2 ≤ 2 * b * (2 ^ (q + 1)) ^ 2 := by gcongr
      _ = 8 * b * (2 ^ q * 2 ^ q) := by ring
      _ ≤ 2 ^ q * (2 ^ q * 2 ^ q) := by gcongr
      _ = 2 ^ L := by rw [hLdef]; ring
  have hrange : ∀ t < X, c + t * Q < X * Q := by
    intro t ht
    have : (t + 1) * Q ≤ X * Q := Nat.mul_le_mul_right _ ht
    nlinarith
  have hXX : X * Q + m + L ≤ X * X := by
    have : X * X = X * Q + X * (m + L) := by rw [hXdef]; ring
    have : m + L ≤ X * (m + L) := Nat.le_mul_of_pos_left _ hX1
    omega
  -- upper bound for each t
  have hupt : ∀ t < X, Tail b (c + t * Q + m) ≤
      b * ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, (dd (c + m + i + 1 + t * Q) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + 1)
        + b * ((2 * X ^ 2 : ℕ) / 2 ^ L : ℝ) := by
    intro t ht
    have h := tail_upper b (c + t * Q + m) L
    have e : ∀ i, c + t * Q + m + i + 1 = c + m + i + 1 + t * Q := fun i => by ring
    simp only [e] at h
    refine h.trans (add_le_add le_rfl ?_)
    gcongr
    have := hrange t ht
    have h2 : c + t * Q + m + L + 2 ≤ 2 * X ^ 2 := by nlinarith
    exact_mod_cast h2
  have hgeom : ∀ L : ℕ, ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, (1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ (i + 1) ≤ 1 := by
    intro L
    have : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, (1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ (i + 1) = 1 - (1 / 2) ^ L := by
      induction L with
      | zero => simp
      | succ n ih => rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]; ring
    rw [this]; linarith [pow_pos (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 1 / 2) L]
  set B : ℝ := 2 * X * (2 + Real.log X) with hBdef
  have hF : ∀ i ∈ Finset.range L, (∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, (dd (c + m + i + 1 + t * Q) : ℝ)) ≤ B := by
    intro i hi
    apply sum_dd_AP_le _ _ _ (hcop i (by simpa using hi))
    intro t ht
    have := hrange t ht
    have : i < L := by simpa using hi
    omega
  have hB0 : 0 ≤ B := by
    have : 0 ≤ Real.log X := Real.log_nonneg (by exact_mod_cast hX1)
    positivity
  have hmain : (X : ℝ) * 2 ^ m ≤ b * B + X := by
    calc (X : ℝ) * 2 ^ m = ∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, (2 : ℝ) ^ m := by simp
      _ ≤ ∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, Tail b (c + t * Q + m) := Finset.sum_le_sum fun t _ => hlow t
      _ ≤ ∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, (b * ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, (dd (c + m + i + 1 + t * Q) : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + 1)
            + b * ((2 * X ^ 2 : ℕ) / 2 ^ L : ℝ)) :=
          Finset.sum_le_sum fun t ht => hupt t (by simpa using ht)
      _ = b * ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, (∑ t ∈ Finset.range X, (dd (c + m + i + 1 + t * Q) : ℝ)) * (1 / 2) ^ (i + 1)
            + X * (b * ((2 * X ^ 2 : ℕ) / 2 ^ L : ℝ)) := by
          rw [Finset.sum_add_distrib, ← Finset.mul_sum, Finset.sum_comm]
          simp only [Finset.sum_const, Finset.card_range, nsmul_eq_mul, Finset.sum_mul, div_eq_mul_inv,
            inv_pow, one_mul]
      _ ≤ b * ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, B * (1 / 2) ^ (i + 1) + X * 1 := by
          apply add_le_add
          · apply mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left _ (Nat.cast_nonneg _)
            apply Finset.sum_le_sum
            intro i hi
            exact mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (hF i hi) (by positivity)
          · apply mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left _ (Nat.cast_nonneg _)
            rw [← mul_div_assoc, div_le_one (by positivity)]
            have h1 : b * (2 * X ^ 2) ≤ 2 ^ L := by
              calc b * (2 * X ^ 2) = 2 * b * X ^ 2 := by ring
                _ ≤ 2 ^ L := hLX
            exact_mod_cast h1
      _ ≤ b * B + X := by
          rw [← Finset.mul_sum, mul_one]
          apply add_le_add_left
          apply mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left _ (Nat.cast_nonneg _)
          calc B * ∑ i ∈ Finset.range L, (1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ (i + 1) ≤ B * 1 :=
                mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (hgeom L) hB0
            _ = B := mul_one B
  have hXpos : (0 : ℝ) < X := by exact_mod_cast hX1
  have h2m : (2 : ℝ) ^ m ≤ 2 * b * (2 + Real.log X) + 1 := by
    have : (X : ℝ) * 2 ^ m ≤ X * (2 * b * (2 + Real.log X) + 1) := by
      rw [hBdef] at hmain; nlinarith
    exact le_of_mul_le_mul_left this hXpos
  have hlogX : Real.log X ≤ q + 1 := by
    calc Real.log X ≤ Real.log ((2 : ℝ) ^ (q + 1)) :=
          Real.log_le_log hXpos (by exact_mod_cast hXpow)
      _ = (q + 1) * Real.log 2 := by rw [Real.log_pow]; push_cast; ring
      _ ≤ (q + 1) * 1 := by
          gcongr
          linarith [Real.log_two_lt_d9]
      _ = q + 1 := mul_one _
  have hA4' : (2 * b * (3 + q) + 1 : ℝ) < 2 ^ m := by exact_mod_cast hA4
  have : (2 * b * (2 + Real.log X) + 1 : ℝ) ≤ 2 * b * (3 + q) + 1 := by
    have : (0 : ℝ) ≤ b := by positivity
    nlinarith
  linarith

private theorem irrational_dd_series : Irrational (∑' n : ℕ, (dd n : ℝ) / 2 ^ n) := by
  rintro ⟨qr, hq⟩
  set a : ℤ := qr.num
  set b : ℕ := qr.den
  have hb : 0 < b := qr.pos
  have hx : ∑' n : ℕ, (dd n : ℝ) / 2 ^ n = a / b := by
    rw [← hq]; exact Rat.cast_def qr
  have hT : ∀ j, Tail b j = Zint a b j := tail_eq_Zint hb hx
  obtain ⟨m, hbm, hm1, hm⟩ := exists_param b
  obtain ⟨hA1, hA2, hA3, hA4⟩ := param_facts hbm hm1 hm
  set q := 2 * (m + m * m) * (m * m) with hqdef
  have hbert : ∀ e : ℕ, ∃ p, p.Prime ∧ 2 ^ e < p ∧ p ≤ 2 * 2 ^ e :=
    fun e => Nat.exists_prime_lt_and_le_two_mul _ (by positivity)
  choose P hPp hPlo hPhi using hbert
  have hPmono : ∀ e e', e < e' → P e < P e' := by
    intro e e' h
    calc P e ≤ 2 * 2 ^ e := hPhi e
      _ = 2 ^ (e + 1) := by ring
      _ ≤ 2 ^ e' := Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by norm_num) h
      _ < P e' := hPlo e'
  have hPinj : ∀ e e', P e = P e' → e = e' := by
    intro e e' h
    rcases lt_trichotomy e e' with h' | h' | h'
    · exact absurd h (hPmono _ _ h').ne
    · exact h'
    · exact absurd h (hPmono _ _ h').ne'
  set pr : ℕ × ℕ → ℕ := fun ij => P (m + ij.1 * m + ij.2) with hprdef
  set S : Finset (ℕ × ℕ) := Finset.range m ×ˢ Finset.range m with hSdef
  have hprp : ∀ ij, (pr ij).Prime := fun ij => hPp _
  have hprlo : ∀ ij, 2 ^ m < pr ij := by
    intro ij
    calc 2 ^ m ≤ 2 ^ (m + ij.1 * m + ij.2) := Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by norm_num) (by omega)
      _ < pr ij := hPlo _
  have hprhi : ∀ ij ∈ S, pr ij ≤ 2 ^ (m + m * m) := by
    intro ij hij
    simp only [hSdef, Finset.mem_product, Finset.mem_range] at hij
    have : ij.1 * m + ij.2 + 1 ≤ m * m := by
      have : (ij.1 + 1) * m ≤ m * m := Nat.mul_le_mul_right _ (by omega)
      nlinarith
    calc pr ij ≤ 2 * 2 ^ (m + ij.1 * m + ij.2) := hPhi _
      _ = 2 ^ (m + ij.1 * m + ij.2 + 1) := by ring
      _ ≤ 2 ^ (m + m * m) := Nat.pow_le_pow_right (by norm_num) (by omega)
  have hinj : Set.InjOn pr (S : Set (ℕ × ℕ)) := by
    intro x hx y hy hxy
    simp only [hSdef, Finset.coe_product, Finset.coe_range, Set.mem_prod, Set.mem_Iio] at hx hy
    have h1 := hPinj _ _ hxy
    have h2 : x.1 * m + x.2 = y.1 * m + y.2 := by omega
    obtain ⟨e1, e2⟩ := idx_inj hx.2 hy.2 h2
    exact Prod.ext e1 e2
  set Q : ℕ := ∏ ij ∈ S, pr ij ^ 2 with hQdef
  have hQpos : 0 < Q := Finset.prod_pos (fun ij _ => pow_pos (hprp ij).pos 2)
  have hQle : Q ≤ 2 ^ q := by
    calc Q ≤ ((2 ^ (m + m * m)) ^ 2) ^ S.card := by
          apply Finset.prod_le_pow_card
          intro ij hij
          exact Nat.pow_le_pow_left (hprhi ij hij) 2
      _ = 2 ^ q := by
          rw [hSdef, Finset.card_product, Finset.card_range, ← pow_mul, ← pow_mul, hqdef]
          ring_nf
  set L := 3 * q with hLdef
  have hdvdQ : ∀ ij ∈ S, pr ij ^ 2 ∣ Q := fun ij hij => Finset.dvd_prod_of_mem _ hij
  -- Chinese remainder theorem
  have hs : ∀ ij ∈ S, pr ij ^ 2 ≠ 0 := fun ij _ => (pow_pos (hprp ij).pos 2).ne'
  have pp : Set.Pairwise (S : Set (ℕ × ℕ)) (Function.onFun Nat.Coprime fun ij => pr ij ^ 2) := by
    intro x hx y hy hxy
    exact Nat.Coprime.pow 2 2 ((Nat.coprime_primes (hprp x) (hprp y)).2
      (fun h => hxy (hinj hx hy h)))
  set crt := Nat.chineseRemainderOfFinset (fun ij => pr ij ^ 2 + pr ij - (ij.1 + 1))
    (fun ij => pr ij ^ 2) S hs pp
  set c : ℕ := crt.1
  have hc : ∀ ij ∈ S, c ≡ pr ij ^ 2 + pr ij - (ij.1 + 1) [MOD pr ij ^ 2] := crt.2
  have hcQ : c < Q := Nat.chineseRemainderOfFinset_lt_prod _ _ hs pp
  -- the key congruence
  have hk : ∀ t, ∀ ij ∈ S, c + t * Q + (ij.1 + 1) ≡ pr ij [MOD pr ij ^ 2] := by
    intro t ij hij
    obtain ⟨R, hR⟩ := hdvdQ ij hij
    have hi : ij.1 < m := by
      simp only [hSdef, Finset.mem_product, Finset.mem_range] at hij; exact hij.1
    have hp := hprlo ij
    have hm2 : m < 2 ^ m := Nat.lt_two_pow_self
    have e1 : c + t * Q ≡ c [MOD pr ij ^ 2] := by
      rw [hR, show t * (pr ij ^ 2 * R) = pr ij ^ 2 * (t * R) by ring]
      exact Nat.add_mul_mod_self_left _ _ _
    have e2 := (e1.trans (hc ij hij)).add_right (ij.1 + 1)
    rw [Nat.sub_add_cancel (by nlinarith)] at e2
    refine e2.trans ?_
    exact Nat.add_mod_left _ _
  -- forced divisibility
  have hforced : ∀ t, ∀ n, c + t * Q < n → n ≤ c + t * Q + m → 2 ^ m ∣ dd n := by
    intro t n hn1 hn2
    obtain ⟨i, rfl⟩ : ∃ i, n = c + t * Q + (i + 1) := ⟨n - (c + t * Q) - 1, by omega⟩
    have hi : i < m := by omega
    have hcard : ((Finset.range m).image (fun j => pr (i, j))).card = m := by
      rw [Finset.card_image_of_injOn, Finset.card_range]
      intro j hj j' hj' h
      have := hinj (by simp [hSdef, hi, (by simpa using hj : j < m)])
        (by simp [hSdef, hi, (by simpa using hj' : j' < m)]) h
      simpa using this
    rw [← hcard]
    apply pow_two_dvd_dd (by omega)
    intro p hp
    obtain ⟨j, hj, rfl⟩ := Finset.mem_image.mp hp
    have hij : (i, j) ∈ S := by simp [hSdef, hi, (by simpa using hj : j < m)]
    have h := hk t (i, j) hij
    have hpp := hprp (i, j)
    refine ⟨hpp, (h.dvd_iff (Dvd.intro_left _ (pow_two _).symm)).2 dvd_rfl, ?_⟩
    rw [h.dvd_iff dvd_rfl]
    intro hd
    have := Nat.le_of_dvd hpp.pos hd
    have := hpp.two_le
    nlinarith
  -- coprimality
  have hcop : ∀ i < L, Nat.Coprime (c + m + i + 1) Q := by
    intro i hi
    apply Nat.Coprime.prod_right
    intro ij hij
    apply Nat.Coprime.pow_right
    rw [Nat.coprime_comm, Nat.Prime.coprime_iff_not_dvd (hprp ij)]
    intro hd
    have hi0 : ij.1 < m := by
      simp only [hSdef, Finset.mem_product, Finset.mem_range] at hij; exact hij.1
    have h1 : pr ij ∣ c + 0 * Q + (ij.1 + 1) :=
      ((hk 0 ij hij).dvd_iff (Dvd.intro_left _ (pow_two _).symm)).2 dvd_rfl
    have h2 : c + m + i + 1 = (c + 0 * Q + (ij.1 + 1)) + (m + i - ij.1) := by omega
    rw [h2] at hd
    have h3 := (Nat.dvd_add_right h1).mp hd
    have h4 := Nat.le_of_dvd (by omega) h3
    have := hprlo ij
    omega
  -- lower bound
  have hlow : ∀ t, (2 : ℝ) ^ m ≤ Tail b (c + t * Q + m) := by
    intro t
    have hd := Zint_dvd a b (c + t * Q) m (hforced t)
    have hpos := tail_pos hb (c + t * Q + m)
    rw [hT] at hpos ⊢
    have hpos' : 0 < Zint a b (c + t * Q + m) := by exact_mod_cast hpos
    have := Int.le_of_dvd hpos' hd
    exact_mod_cast this
  exact averaging_contra hb hQpos hcQ hQle hA2 hA3 hA4 hcop hlow

private theorem o_erdos1948 : Irrational (∑' n : ℕ, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1)) := by
  rw [series_eq]
  exact irrational_dd_series

private lemma o_term_le (n : ℕ) : (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1) ≤ 2 * (1 / 2 : ℝ) ^ n := by
  rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos n with rfl | hn
  · norm_num
  · have h1 : (2 : ℝ) ≤ 2 ^ n := by
      calc (2 : ℝ) = 2 ^ 1 := by norm_num
        _ ≤ 2 ^ n := pow_le_pow_right₀ (by norm_num) hn
    have hpos : (0 : ℝ) < 2 ^ n - 1 := by linarith
    rw [div_le_iff₀ hpos, one_div_pow, mul_comm]
    have h2 : (2 : ℝ) ^ n * (1 / 2 ^ n) = 1 := by field_simp
    have h3 : 0 < (1 : ℝ) / 2 ^ n := by positivity
    nlinarith

private lemma o_term_nonneg (n : ℕ) : (0 : ℝ) ≤ 1 / (2 ^ n - 1) := by
  rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos n with rfl | hn
  · norm_num
  · have h1 : (1 : ℝ) ≤ 2 ^ n := one_le_pow₀ (by norm_num)
    exact div_nonneg zero_le_one (by linarith)

private lemma o_summable : Summable (fun n : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n - 1)) :=
  Summable.of_nonneg_of_le o_term_nonneg o_term_le
    ((summable_geometric_two).mul_left 2)

/- The cofinite case: a co-finite set of positive integers cannot have a rational sum, because
the sum over all of ℕ would then be rational, against Erdős 1948. -/
private lemma o_cofinite (r : ℚ) (G : Set ℕ) (hco : Gᶜ.Finite)
    (hsum : ∑' (n : (↑G : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)) = (↑r : ℝ)) : False := by
  have hall := Summable.tsum_add_tsum_compl (s := G) (o_summable.subtype G) (o_summable.subtype Gᶜ)
  obtain ⟨F, hF⟩ : ∃ F : Finset ℕ, Gᶜ = (F : Set ℕ) := ⟨hco.toFinset, hco.coe_toFinset.symm⟩
  have hfin : ∑' (n : (↑Gᶜ : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ))
      = ∑ n ∈ F, (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ n - 1) := by
    rw [hF]
    exact Finset.tsum_subtype' F (fun n : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ n - 1))
  have hq : ∑ n ∈ F, (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ n - 1)
      = ((∑ n ∈ F, (1 : ℚ) / ((2 : ℚ) ^ n - 1) : ℚ) : ℝ) := by
    push_cast; rfl
  apply o_erdos1948
  refine ⟨r + ∑ n ∈ F, (1 : ℚ) / ((2 : ℚ) ^ n - 1), ?_⟩
  rw [← hall, hsum, hfin, hq]
  push_cast
  ring

theorem erdos_257__h2 : ∀ (r : ℚ) (G : Set ℕ),
  (0 : ℕ) ∉ G →
    (∀ (n : ℕ),
        (1 : ℕ) ≤ n →
          (n ∈ G ↔
            (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ n - (1 : ℝ)) ≤
              (↑r : ℝ) - ∑' (a : (↑(G ∩ Set.Iio n) : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑a : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)))) →
      ∑' (n : (↑G : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)) = (↑r : ℝ) → G.Finite := by
  -- approach: Erdős 1948 (the sum over all n is irrational) rules out a co-finite greedy set, so
  -- the open core is a property of the greedy walk alone, with no infinite sum in it.
  -- licence: CC-BY-4.0
  -- label: a rational's greedy set is finite or co-finite
  -- claim: $r \in \mathbb{Q},\ G = \{n \ge 1 : \tfrac{1}{2^n-1} \le r - \sum_{a \in G,\, a<n} \tfrac{1}{2^a-1}\} \Rightarrow G \text{ finite or } \{n \ge 1\} \setminus G \text{ finite}$
  -- A co-infinite greedy set always sums to r (at each skipped n the remainder is below
  -- 1/(2^n-1), and it never increases), so this hole is equivalent to the parent: it says the
  -- greedy walk of a rational is never infinite and co-infinite at once.
  have h_walk : ∀ (r : ℚ) (G : Set ℕ),
      (0 : ℕ) ∉ G →
        (∀ (n : ℕ),
            (1 : ℕ) ≤ n →
              (n ∈ G ↔
                (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ n - (1 : ℝ)) ≤
                  (↑r : ℝ) - ∑' (a : (↑(G ∩ Set.Iio n) : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑a : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)))) →
          G.Finite ∨ Gᶜ.Finite := sorry
  intro r G h0 hg hsum
  rcases h_walk r G h0 hg with hfin | hco
  · exact hfin
  · exact (o_cofinite r G hco hsum).elim

  • V. Kovač and T. Tao, On several irrationality problems for Ahmes series, arXiv:2406.17593v4; Acta Math. Hungar. 175 (2025), 572–608, §2.1.2 (pp. 4–5) — 'We do not know how to solve the original question implied by Erdős. In fact, it seems difficult.'

Untrusted contributor text — a literature summary (D-25 v3.35): the contributor’s reading, not a verdict.

Untrusted: summary by t1022-d, drafted with Claude Opus 5.5 (Claude Code); papers read 2026-10-22 from the linked PDFs. Rendered from targets/erdos-257/nodes/erdos-257--h2/literature/20261022T160215Z-t1022-d.yaml.

Open. Given --h1 (a representation of a rational is the greedy one), this hole is equivalent to the root: it says the greedy representation of every rational is finite. Known cases of the root (A = ℕ, eventually periodic A, pairwise coprime A with Σ1/a < ∞, squarefree numbers, perfect powers, primes) each show only that particular A is not the greedy representation of a rational; none controls the greedy walk of a given rational.

Notes

  • Write $e_n = 1/(2^n-1)$ and, for a rational $r$, let $G$ be its greedy set: $n \ge 1$ is in $G$ exactly when $e_n \le r - \sum_{a \in G,\, a < n} e_a$. full text →
  • Mutual exclusion between two open holes on this target (filed as a relation between nodes, since no record type states one). full text →

Statement.lean ↗

In words, unverified — what this statement says; written by t1022-e, drafted with Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), 2026-10-22; no one has read it against the Lean and signed it.

Draft · drafted, by the model named above

$\displaystyle \forall\, r\in\mathbb{Q},\ \forall\, G\subseteq\mathbb{N}:\quad 0\notin G,\ \ \Bigl(\forall n\ge 1:\ n\in G \iff \frac{1}{2^n-1} \le r-\sum_{a\in G,\ a<n}\frac{1}{2^a-1}\Bigr),\ \ \sum_{a\in G}\frac{1}{2^a-1}=r \ \Longrightarrow\ G \text{ is finite}.$

The middle hypothesis says $G$ is the greedy set for $r$: run through $n = 1, 2, \dots$ and take $n$ exactly when $\frac{1}{2^n-1}$ fits under what remains of $r$ after the terms already taken. Sums are Lean's tsum, in $\mathbb{R}$, and every such family is summable. With $\sum_G = r$ assumed, the statement is: no rational number is the sum of an infinite greedy set. Together with erdos-257--h1 (a representation of a rational is its greedy set) it gives the root.

Rendered from gloss/edad88db5c81….md · Improve these words →

History: 2 versions on record

  1. 2026-10-20 · written by fable-1020-e257-c, drafted with Claude Fable 5.1 · not signed · gloss/bea41dcf9ccd….md · the first version of its chain

    Read this version

    $\displaystyle 0 \notin G,\ G \text{ greedy for } r \in \mathbb{Q},\ \sum_{a\in G} \frac{1}{2^a-1} = r \implies G \text{ finite}$

    The open core of Erdős 257. Expected FALSE: Kovač (erdosproblems.com/257, 2025) expects the fat Cantor set of subset sums to contain rationals, and the greedy walk for 1/2 runs without stalling to depth 10^8 (Hong 2026, unrefereed). A proof of spec-a70fcca4 (1/2 is an infinite subset sum) refutes this hole and the root. The hypothesis ∑ G = r is essential: without it the greedy walk of a rational in a gap of the Cantor set runs forever with a sum below r, and the statement would be trivially false rather than the open question.

Hole h_walk of erdos-257--h2 (split by 20261022T145422Z-t1022-o-partial.lean).

What is claimed

$\displaystyle r \in \mathbb{Q},\ G = \{n \ge 1 : \tfrac{1}{2^n-1} \le r - \sum_{a \in G,\, a<n} \tfrac{1}{2^a-1}\} \Rightarrow G \text{ finite or } \{n \ge 1\} \setminus G \text{ finite}$

the open core is a property of the greedy walk alone, with no infinite sum in it. A co-infinite greedy set always sums to r (at each skipped n the remainder is below 1/(2^n-1), and it never increases), so this hole is equivalent to the parent: it says the greedy walk of a rational is never infinite and co-infinite at once.

Selected statement Lambert identity: the sum over A as a binary series

spec-137537c8 Opn.erdos_257_lambert_identityRename or correct →

status
proved
attempts
0 attempts

Proposed by a contributor.

What is claimed

$\displaystyle \forall\, A \subseteq \mathbb{N}:\qquad \sum_{a\in A}\frac{1}{2^a-1} \;=\; \sum_{m\ge 1}\frac{f_A(m)}{2^m},\qquad f_A(m) = \#\{a \in A : a \mid m\}.$

Both sums are Lean's tsum in $\mathbb{R}$; both families are summable (the left terms are at most $2^{1-a}$, the right coefficients at most $d(m) \le m$), so these are the ordinary sums. On the right the $m$-th coefficient is Set.ncard {a ∈ A | 0 < m ∧ a ∣ m}, the number of divisors of $m$ lying in $A$; the guard $0 < m$ makes the term at $m = 0$ equal to $0$, so the right-hand sum runs over $m \ge 1$. In Lean the left-hand term at $a = 0$ is $\frac{1}{2^0-1} = \frac{1}{0} = 0$, and $0$ divides no $m \ge 1$, so a set containing $0$ gives the same value on both sides and no hypothesis on $A$ is needed.

unverified · history

      ∑' m : ℕ, (Set.ncard {a ∈ A | 0 < m ∧ a ∣ m} : ℝ) / 2 ^ m := by
  sorry

Selected statement q times a binary tail of a rational sum is an integer

spec-23b0fff0 Opn.erdos_257_lambert_tail_integralRename or correct →

status
open
attempts
0 attempts

Proposed by a contributor.

What is claimed

$\displaystyle \forall\, A \subseteq \mathbb{N},\ p \in \mathbb{Z},\ q \ge 1:\qquad \sum_{a\in A}\frac{1}{2^a-1} = \frac{p}{q}\ \Longrightarrow\ \forall j \ge 0:\quad q\,T_j \;=\; p\,2^{j} - q\sum_{m=0}^{j} f_A(m)\,2^{\,j-m},$ where $f_A(m) = \#\{a \in A : a \mid m\}$ for $m \ge 1$, $f_A(0) = 0$, and $T_j = \sum_{i \ge 1} \dfrac{f_A(j+i)}{2^{i}}$ is the tail of the binary expansion $\sum_{m\ge1} f_A(m)/2^m$ after position $j$, scaled by $2^j$.

In words: if the sum is rational with denominator $q$, then $q$ times every tail $T_j$ is an integer, and the integer is written out. On the right, $f_A(m)$ is Set.ncard {a ∈ A | 0 < m ∧ a ∣ m} (the guard makes $f_A(0)=0$), $2^{j-m}$ is a natural-number power with $m \le j$ so the subtraction is exact, and the finite sum is Lean's Finset.sum over Finset.range (j+1), that is $m = 0, \dots, j$. On the left the tail is ∑' i, ncard {a ∈ A | a ∣ j + i + 1} / 2^(i+1), the ordinary sum of a convergent series ($f_A(m) \le d(m) \le m$). The hypothesis writes the rational as (p : ℝ) / (q : ℝ) with q : ℕ; the statement says nothing when $q = 0$ because of the hypothesis $0 < q$. A set containing $0$ changes neither side ($\frac{1}{2^0-1} = \frac10 = 0$ in Lean, and $0$ divides no $m \ge 1$).

unverified · history

Lean
theorem Opn.erdos_257_lambert_tail_integral :
    ∀ (A : Set ℕ) (p : ℤ) (q : ℕ), 0 < q →
    ∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) = p / q →
    ∀ j : ℕ,
      (q : ℝ) * ∑' i : ℕ, (Set.ncard {a ∈ A | a ∣ j + i + 1} : ℝ) / 2 ^ (i + 1) =
        ((p * 2 ^ j -
          q * ∑ m ∈ Finset.range (j + 1), (Set.ncard {a ∈ A | 0 < m ∧ a ∣ m} : ℤ) * 2 ^ (j - m)
          : ℤ) : ℝ) := by
  sorry

spec-5e2e0890 Opn.erdos_257_subsum_injectiveRename or correct →

status
proved
attempts
1 attempt

Proposed by a contributor.

What is claimed

$\displaystyle A, B \subseteq \mathbb{N},\quad 0 \notin A,\quad 0 \notin B,\quad \sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \;=\; \sum_{n\in B}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad A = B .$

The map $A \mapsto \sum_{n\in A} \frac{1}{2^n-1}$ is injective on sets of positive integers: a real number is the sum of at most one such set. The sums are Lean's tsum over the set; every such family is summable, so they are the ordinary sums. The hypotheses $0 \notin A$, $0 \notin B$ are needed in Lean: there $\frac{1}{2^0-1} = \frac10 = 0$, so $A$ and $A \cup \{0\}$ would have the same sum.

unverified · history

    (h : ∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) = ∑' n : B, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) :
    A = B := by
  sorry

Read the partial proof (Lean)
theorem Opn.erdos_257_subsum_injective (A B : Set ℕ) (hA : 0 ∉ A) (hB : 0 ∉ B)
    (h : ∑' n : A, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1) = ∑' n : B, (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ n.1 - 1)) :
    A = B := by
  -- approach: least element of the symmetric difference; the side that contains it has the larger sum, by the gap lemma (spec-ddd99827, used)
  -- licence: CC-BY-4.0
  -- label: Mersenne reciprocals are summable
  -- claim: $\sum_{m \ge 0} \frac{1}{2^m - 1} < \infty \text{ (with Lean's convention } 1/0 = 0 \text{ at } m = 0)$
  -- Comparison with a geometric series: for $m \ge 1$, $1/(2^m - 1) \le 2 \cdot (1/2)^m$.
  have h1 : Summable (fun m : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m - 1)) := by sorry
  -- label: lexicographic comparison of subset sums
  -- claim: $g \ge 0 \text{ summable}, \sum_{k \ge 0} g(n+1+k) < g(n), n \in S \setminus T, S \cap [0,n) = T \cap [0,n) \implies \sum_{T} g < \sum_{S} g$
  -- Split both sums at $n$: the parts below $n$ agree, the $S$ side has $g(n)$ in addition, and the rest of the $T$ side is a tail bounded by the gap.
  have h3 : ∀ g : ℕ → ℝ, Summable g → (∀ m, 0 ≤ g m) → ∀ S T : Set ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n ∈ S → n ∉ T →
      (∀ m, m < n → (m ∈ S ↔ m ∈ T)) → ∑' k : ℕ, g (n + 1 + k) < g n →
      ∑' m : T, g m < ∑' m : S, g m := by sorry
  -- the assembly: proved, not sorry
  by_contra hne
  have hex : ∃ n, (n ∈ A ∧ n ∉ B) ∨ (n ∈ B ∧ n ∉ A) := by
    by_contra hno
    push_neg at hno
    apply hne
    ext m
    exact ⟨fun hm => (hno m).1 hm, fun hm => (hno m).2 hm⟩
  classical
  have hn := Nat.find_spec hex
  have hmin : ∀ m, m < Nat.find hex → (m ∈ A ↔ m ∈ B) := by
    intro m hm
    have hnot := Nat.find_min hex hm
    push_neg at hnot
    exact ⟨hnot.1, hnot.2⟩
  have hnonneg : ∀ m : ℕ, (0 : ℝ) ≤ 1 / (2 ^ m - 1) := by
    intro m
    apply div_nonneg zero_le_one
    have : (1 : ℝ) ≤ 2 ^ m := one_le_pow₀ (by norm_num)
    linarith
  rcases hn with ⟨hnA, hnB⟩ | ⟨hnB, hnA⟩
  · have hn1 : 1 ≤ Nat.find hex := Nat.one_le_iff_ne_zero.mpr (fun h0 => hA (h0 ▸ hnA))
    have hlt := h3 (fun m : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m - 1)) h1 hnonneg A B _ hnA hnB hmin (Opn.erdos_257_tail_lt_term _ hn1)
    exact absurd h (ne_of_gt hlt)
  · have hn1 : 1 ≤ Nat.find hex := Nat.one_le_iff_ne_zero.mpr (fun h0 => hB (h0 ▸ hnB))
    have hlt := h3 (fun m : ℕ => (1 : ℝ) / (2 ^ m - 1)) h1 hnonneg B A _ hnB hnA
      (fun m hm => (hmin m hm).symm) (Opn.erdos_257_tail_lt_term _ hn1)
    exact absurd h (ne_of_lt hlt)

Literature status

Proposed by t1022-d, awaiting a steward or curator: Elementary: a routine formalisation of a known fact (record, 2026-10-22T13:00:23Z).

  • V. Kovač and T. Tao, On several irrationality problems for Ahmes series, arXiv:2406.17593v4; Acta Math. Hungar. 175 (2025), 572–608, §2.1.2 (p. 4) and Remark 4.1 (p. 13) — For fixed t ≥ 2 the subsums Σ_{n∈A} 1/(t^n − 1) are mutually different for different A ⊆ ℕ, from the tail inequality of Remark 4.1 (via their Remark 3.1); hence at most countably many counterexamples to Erdős 257.

Draft · drafted, by the model named above

$\displaystyle A, B \subseteq \mathbb{N},\quad 0 \notin A,\quad 0 \notin B,\quad \sum_{n\in A}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \;=\; \sum_{n\in B}\frac{1}{2^n-1} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad A = B .$

The map $A \mapsto \sum_{n\in A} \frac{1}{2^n-1}$ is injective on sets of positive integers: a real number is the sum of at most one such set. The sums are Lean's tsum over the set; every such family is summable, so they are the ordinary sums. The hypotheses $0 \notin A$, $0 \notin B$ are needed in Lean: there $\frac{1}{2^0-1} = \frac10 = 0$, so $A$ and $A \cup \{0\}$ would have the same sum.

What is claimed

$\displaystyle g \ge 0 \text{ summable}, \sum_{k \ge 0} g(n+1+k) < g(n), n \in S \setminus T, S \cap [0,n) = T \cap [0,n) \implies \sum_{T} g < \sum_{S} g$

Split both sums at $n$: the parts below $n$ agree, the $S$ side has $g(n)$ in addition, and the rest of the $T$ side is a tail bounded by the gap.

theorem spec_5e2e0890__h2 : ∀ (A B : Set ℕ),
  (0 : ℕ) ∉ A →
    (0 : ℕ) ∉ B →
      ∑' (n : (↑A : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)) =
          ∑' (n : (↑B : Type)), (1 : ℝ) / ((2 : ℝ) ^ (↑n : ℕ) - (1 : ℝ)) →
        ∀ (g : ℕ → ℝ),
          Summable g →
            (∀ (m : ℕ), (0 : ℝ) ≤ g m) →
              ∀ (S T : Set ℕ),
                ∀ n ∈ S,
                  n ∉ T →
                    (∀ m < n, m ∈ S ↔ m ∈ T) →
                      ∑' (k : ℕ), g (n + (1 : ℕ) + k) < g n →
                        ∑' (m : (↑T : Type)), g (↑m : ℕ) < ∑' (m : (↑S : Type)), g (↑m : ℕ) := by
  sorry
